People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I’d like to think I would’ve been okay if that hadn’t happened… but I can’t say for certain. It just seems like the timing lines up, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think so. I try not to make a big thing of it, but a lot of mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like the color’s gone out of the house, if that makes sense. I usually just try to pull myself together so I don’t break down in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has changed?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, some days I’m not as interested in food, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I haven’t really been keeping track or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think it’s been… terrible every night or anything. Some nights I’m awake longer than I’d like, but I haven’t really been keeping track. Should I be?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, there have been times before where I felt pretty low for stretches. I don’t know if I’d call it the same exactly, but that heavy, empty feeling in the mornings—like everything’s kind of gone gray—and having to hold it together so I don’t fall apart in front of my wife… that’s happened before. For days at a time, maybe longer. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Before this started two months ago, have you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there have been stretches before where most mornings I’d wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like the color had gone out of the house, if that makes sense. I’d just try to pull myself together so I didn’t break down in front of my wife. So… yes, I think that probably fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-month period, did you ever have a separate episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks and then fully lifted before this one began?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I know I’ve had low stretches before, but whether it was a full two weeks and then completely lifted before this… I don’t know. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. It would ease up some, I think, but I’m not sure I’d call it fully gone.\n\nDoctor: During this past two months, have you lost interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just sitting around doing nothing, but the things that usually keep me steady just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nSalsa nights, church gatherings, cooking something for the neighbors… even walking into my shop just to enjoy the smell of the wood, which used to be one of my favorite things. Lately I just don’t really want to do any of it. It’s like the enjoyment part isn’t there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to manage your usual responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has. I’ve tried to keep things going, and I don’t want to sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but work’s been harder than I’d like to admit.\n\nThere are parts of jobs I’d normally supervise myself, and lately I’ve had to hand those off to other guys because I just can’t seem to stay on top of it the way I should.\n\nAnd outside of work… I’ve pulled back a lot. Church, friends, neighbors — I keep telling myself I’m just tired or busy, but I guess it’s been enough that my wife got worried. Even someone from the parish reached out because they were concerned about my safety. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s been affecting my responsibilities day to day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess I have had thoughts like that. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to scare anyone, or that I’m being dramatic.\n\nIt’s more like… sometimes I think maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I hate admitting this, but sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me when it happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

Some nights I’m awake longer than I’d like, but I haven’t really been keeping track. Should I be?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, there have been times before where I felt pretty low for stretches. I don’t know if I’d call it the same exactly, but that heavy, empty feeling in the mornings—like everything’s kind of gone gray—and having to hold it together so I don’t fall apart in front of my wife… that’s happened before. For days at a time, maybe longer. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Before this started two months ago, have you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there have been stretches before where most mornings I’d wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like the color had gone out of the house, if that makes sense. I’d just try to pull myself together so I didn’t break down in front of my wife. So… yes, I think that probably fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-month period, did you ever have a separate episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks and then fully lifted before this one began?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I know I’ve had low stretches before, but whether it was a full two weeks and then completely lifted before this… I don’t know. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. It would ease up some, I think, but I’m not sure I’d call it fully gone.\n\nDoctor: During this past two months, have you lost interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just sitting around doing nothing, but the things that usually keep me steady just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nSalsa nights, church gatherings, cooking something for the neighbors… even walking into my shop just to enjoy the smell of the wood, which used to be one of my favorite things. Lately I just don’t really want to do any of it. It’s like the enjoyment part isn’t there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to manage your usual responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has. I’ve tried to keep things going, and I don’t want to sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but work’s been harder than I’d like to admit.\n\nThere are parts of jobs I’d normally supervise myself, and lately I’ve had to hand those off to other guys because I just can’t seem to stay on top of it the way I should.\n\nAnd outside of work… I’ve pulled back a lot. Church, friends, neighbors — I keep telling myself I’m just tired or busy, but I guess it’s been enough that my wife got worried. Even someone from the parish reached out because they were concerned about my safety. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s been affecting my responsibilities day to day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess I have had thoughts like that. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to scare anyone, or that I’m being dramatic.\n\nIt’s more like… sometimes I think maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I hate admitting this, but sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, not really like that. I mean, I’ve had those thoughts about maybe it being easier if I didn’t wake up, or that my family might somehow be better off without me — and I know that’s not good to think.\n\nBut as far as how I would do it… I don’t think I’ve really gone there. Some nights I’m awake longer than I’d like, but I haven’t really been keeping track. Should I be?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, there have been times before where I felt pretty low for stretches. I don’t know if I’d call it the same exactly, but that heavy, empty feeling in the mornings—like everything’s kind of gone gray—and having to hold it together so I don’t fall apart in front of my wife… that’s happened before. For days at a time, maybe longer. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Before this started two months ago, have you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there have been stretches before where most mornings I’d wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like the color had gone out of the house, if that makes sense. I’d just try to pull myself together so I didn’t break down in front of my wife. So… yes, I think that probably fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-month period, did you ever have a separate episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks and then fully lifted before this one began?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I know I’ve had low stretches before, but whether it was a full two weeks and then completely lifted before this… I don’t know. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. It would ease up some, I think, but I’m not sure I’d call it fully gone.\n\nDoctor: During this past two months, have you lost interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just sitting around doing nothing, but the things that usually keep me steady just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nSalsa nights, church gatherings, cooking something for the neighbors… even walking into my shop just to enjoy the smell of the wood, which used to be one of my favorite things. Lately I just don’t really want to do any of it. It’s like the enjoyment part isn’t there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to manage your usual responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has. I’ve tried to keep things going, and I don’t want to sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but work’s been harder than I’d like to admit.\n\nThere are parts of jobs I’d normally supervise myself, and lately I’ve had to hand those off to other guys because I just can’t seem to stay on top of it the way I should.\n\nAnd outside of work… I’ve pulled back a lot. Church, friends, neighbors — I keep telling myself I’m just tired or busy, but I guess it’s been enough that my wife got worried. Even someone from the parish reached out because they were concerned about my safety. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s been affecting my responsibilities day to day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess I have had thoughts like that. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to scare anyone, or that I’m being dramatic.\n\nIt’s more like… sometimes I think maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I hate admitting this, but sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, not really like that. I mean, I’ve had those thoughts about maybe it being easier if I didn’t wake up, or that my family might somehow be better off without me — and I know that’s not good to think.\n\nBut as far as how I would do it… I don’t think I’ve really gone there. It scares me enough just having the thoughts, honestly. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have, more than I probably want to admit. I keep telling myself I’m just getting older or working too hard, but even when I sit down for a while, or I’ve been in bed all night, my body still feels worn out.\n\nIt’s like I’ve been framing a roof all day, even when I haven’t done much. Little things — like loading tools into the truck — can feel like more than I’ve got in me. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I usually just try to pull myself together so I don’t break down in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has changed?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, some days I’m not as interested in food, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I haven’t really been keeping track or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think it’s been… terrible every night or anything. Some nights I’m awake longer than I’d like, but I haven’t really been keeping track. Should I be?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, there have been times before where I felt pretty low for stretches. I don’t know if I’d call it the same exactly, but that heavy, empty feeling in the mornings—like everything’s kind of gone gray—and having to hold it together so I don’t fall apart in front of my wife… that’s happened before. For days at a time, maybe longer. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Before this started two months ago, have you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there have been stretches before where most mornings I’d wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like the color had gone out of the house, if that makes sense. I’d just try to pull myself together so I didn’t break down in front of my wife. So… yes, I think that probably fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-month period, did you ever have a separate episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks and then fully lifted before this one began?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I know I’ve had low stretches before, but whether it was a full two weeks and then completely lifted before this… I don’t know. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. It would ease up some, I think, but I’m not sure I’d call it fully gone.\n\nDoctor: During this past two months, have you lost interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just sitting around doing nothing, but the things that usually keep me steady just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nSalsa nights, church gatherings, cooking something for the neighbors… even walking into my shop just to enjoy the smell of the wood, which used to be one of my favorite things. Lately I just don’t really want to do any of it. It’s like the enjoyment part isn’t there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to manage your usual responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has. I’ve tried to keep things going, and I don’t want to sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but work’s been harder than I’d like to admit.\n\nThere are parts of jobs I’d normally supervise myself, and lately I’ve had to hand those off to other guys because I just can’t seem to stay on top of it the way I should.\n\nAnd outside of work… I’ve pulled back a lot. Church, friends, neighbors — I keep telling myself I’m just tired or busy, but I guess it’s been enough that my wife got worried. Even someone from the parish reached out because they were concerned about my safety. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s been affecting my responsibilities day to day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess I have had thoughts like that. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to scare anyone, or that I’m being dramatic.\n\nIt’s more like… sometimes I think maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I hate admitting this, but sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, not really like that. I mean, I’ve had those thoughts about maybe it being easier if I didn’t wake up, or that my family might somehow be better off without me — and I know that’s not good to think.\n\nBut as far as how I would do it… I don’t think I’ve really gone there. It scares me enough just having the thoughts, honestly. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have, more than I probably want to admit. I keep telling myself I’m just getting older or working too hard, but even when I sit down for a while, or I’ve been in bed all night, my body still feels worn out.\n\nIt’s like I’ve been framing a roof all day, even when I haven’t done much. Little things — like loading tools into the truck — can feel like more than I’ve got in me. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d say worthless exactly. That sounds pretty harsh.\n\nBut yeah, I’ve been pretty hard on myself. I keep going back over every appointment, every change in my wife’s health, thinking maybe I should’ve noticed something sooner, or pushed harder, or somehow protected her better.\n\nI know that might not be fair, but it feels like… a better husband would’ve caught it. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I just haven’t been myself lately. I don’t seem to want to do the things that usually keep me going, you know? I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there have been stretches before where most mornings I’d wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. So… yes, I think that probably fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-month period, did you ever have a separate episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks and then fully lifted before this one began?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I know I’ve had low stretches before, but whether it was a full two weeks and then completely lifted before this… I don’t know. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. It would ease up some, I think, but I’m not sure I’d call it fully gone.\n\nDoctor: During this past two months, have you lost interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just sitting around doing nothing, but the things that usually keep me steady just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nSalsa nights, church gatherings, cooking something for the neighbors… even walking into my shop just to enjoy the smell of the wood, which used to be one of my favorite things. Lately I just don’t really want to do any of it. It’s like the enjoyment part isn’t there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to manage your usual responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has. I’ve tried to keep things going, and I don’t want to sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but work’s been harder than I’d like to admit.\n\nThere are parts of jobs I’d normally supervise myself, and lately I’ve had to hand those off to other guys because I just can’t seem to stay on top of it the way I should.\n\nAnd outside of work… I’ve pulled back a lot. Church, friends, neighbors — I keep telling myself I’m just tired or busy, but I guess it’s been enough that my wife got worried. Even someone from the parish reached out because they were concerned about my safety. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s been affecting my responsibilities day to day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess I have had thoughts like that. And I hate admitting this, but sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, not really like that. It scares me enough just having the thoughts, honestly. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have, more than I probably want to admit. I keep telling myself I’m just getting older or working too hard, but even when I sit down for a while, or I’ve been in bed all night, my body still feels worn out.\n\nIt’s like I’ve been framing a roof all day, even when I haven’t done much. Little things — like loading tools into the truck — can feel like more than I’ve got in me. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d say worthless exactly. That sounds pretty harsh.\n\nBut yeah, I’ve been pretty hard on myself. I keep going back over every appointment, every change in my wife’s health, thinking maybe I should’ve noticed something sooner, or pushed harder, or somehow protected her better.\n\nI know that might not be fair, but it feels like… a better husband would’ve caught it. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just sitting around doing nothing, but the things that usually keep me steady just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nSalsa nights, church gatherings, cooking something for the neighbors… even walking into my shop just to enjoy the smell of the wood, which used to be one of my favorite things. Lately I just don’t really want to do any of it. It’s like the enjoyment part isn’t there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to manage your usual responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has. I’ve tried to keep things going, and I don’t want to sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but work’s been harder than I’d like to admit.\n\nThere are parts of jobs I’d normally supervise myself, and lately I’ve had to hand those off to other guys because I just can’t seem to stay on top of it the way I should.\n\nAnd outside of work… I’ve pulled back a lot. Church, friends, neighbors — I keep telling myself I’m just tired or busy, but I guess it’s been enough that my wife got worried. Even someone from the parish reached out because they were concerned about my safety. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s been affecting my responsibilities day to day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess I have had thoughts like that. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to scare anyone, or that I’m being dramatic.\n\nIt’s more like… sometimes I think maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I hate admitting this, but sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, not really like that. I mean, I’ve had those thoughts about maybe it being easier if I didn’t wake up, or that my family might somehow be better off without me — and I know that’s not good to think.\n\nBut as far as how I would do it… I don’t think I’ve really gone there. It scares me enough just having the thoughts, honestly. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have, more than I probably want to admit. I keep telling myself I’m just getting older or working too hard, but even when I sit down for a while, or I’ve been in bed all night, my body still feels worn out.\n\nIt’s like I’ve been framing a roof all day, even when I haven’t done much. Little things — like loading tools into the truck — can feel like more than I’ve got in me. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d say worthless exactly. That sounds pretty harsh.\n\nBut yeah, I’ve been pretty hard on myself. I keep going back over every appointment, every change in my wife’s health, thinking maybe I should’ve noticed something sooner, or pushed harder, or somehow protected her better.\n\nI know that might not be fair, but it feels like… a better husband would’ve caught it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect, even given what’s happening with your wife’s health?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe they are. I mean, I know my wife’s health is a real thing to be upset about, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just falling apart for no reason.\n\nBut I do keep replaying every appointment, every little change, like I should’ve caught something sooner. I keep telling myself a better husband would’ve protected her somehow. Salsa nights, church gatherings, cooking something for the neighbors… even walking into my shop just to smell the wood. That used to settle me. Now I kind of avoid it. It feels silly saying it out loud, but that’s what’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It started around when my wife’s health took a sudden turn, and our whole routine kind of got upended. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming that or being dramatic, but… this change in me has been going on about two months now, since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this change in yourself is mostly because of your wife’s health situation?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to put it all on that, but yes, it does seem tied to when her health suddenly got worse and our routine changed. That’s about when I noticed I wasn’t acting like myself. It’s been going on for around two months now, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health had not worsened, do you think you would still be feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I’d like to think I would’ve been okay if that hadn’t happened… but I can’t say for certain. It just seems like the timing lines up, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think so. I try not to make a big thing of it, but a lot of mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like the color’s gone out of the house, if that makes sense. I usually just try to pull myself together so I don’t break down in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has changed?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, some days I’m not as interested in food, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I haven’t really been keeping track or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think it’s been… terrible every night or anything. Some nights I’m awake longer than I’d like, but I haven’t really been keeping track. Should I be?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, there have been times before where I felt pretty low for stretches. I don’t know if I’d call it the same exactly, but that heavy, empty feeling in the mornings—like everything’s kind of gone gray—and having to hold it together so I don’t fall apart in front of my wife… that’s happened before. For days at a time, maybe longer. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Before this started two months ago, have you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but there have been stretches before where most mornings I’d wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like the color had gone out of the house, if that makes sense. I’d just try to pull myself together so I didn’t break down in front of my wife. So… yes, I think that probably fits what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-month period, did you ever have a separate episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks and then fully lifted before this one began?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it that cleanly. I know I’ve had low stretches before, but whether it was a full two weeks and then completely lifted before this… I don’t know. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. It would ease up some, I think, but I’m not sure I’d call it fully gone.\n\nDoctor: During this past two months, have you lost interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just sitting around doing nothing, but the things that usually keep me steady just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nSalsa nights, church gatherings, cooking something for the neighbors… even walking into my shop just to enjoy the smell of the wood, which used to be one of my favorite things. Lately I just don’t really want to do any of it. It’s like the enjoyment part isn’t there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to manage your usual responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has. I’ve tried to keep things going, and I don’t want to sound like I’m falling apart or anything, but work’s been harder than I’d like to admit.\n\nThere are parts of jobs I’d normally supervise myself, and lately I’ve had to hand those off to other guys because I just can’t seem to stay on top of it the way I should.\n\nAnd outside of work… I’ve pulled back a lot. Church, friends, neighbors — I keep telling myself I’m just tired or busy, but I guess it’s been enough that my wife got worried. Even someone from the parish reached out because they were concerned about my safety. I’m not proud of that. But yes, it’s been affecting my responsibilities day to day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, I guess I have had thoughts like that. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to scare anyone, or that I’m being dramatic.\n\nIt’s more like… sometimes I think maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I hate admitting this, but sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you might kill yourself?\n\nPatient: I… no, not really like that. I mean, I’ve had those thoughts about maybe it being easier if I didn’t wake up, or that my family might somehow be better off without me — and I know that’s not good to think.\n\nBut as far as how I would do it… I don’t think I’ve really gone there. It scares me enough just having the thoughts, honestly. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have, more than I probably want to admit. I keep telling myself I’m just getting older or working too hard, but even when I sit down for a while, or I’ve been in bed all night, my body still feels worn out.\n\nIt’s like I’ve been framing a roof all day, even when I haven’t done much. Little things — like loading tools into the truck — can feel like more than I’ve got in me. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d say worthless exactly. That sounds pretty harsh.\n\nBut yeah, I’ve been pretty hard on myself. I keep going back over every appointment, every change in my wife’s health, thinking maybe I should’ve noticed something sooner, or pushed harder, or somehow protected her better.\n\nI know that might not be fair, but it feels like… a better husband would’ve caught it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect, even given what’s happening with your wife’s health?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe they are. I mean, I know my wife’s health is a real thing to be upset about, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just falling apart for no reason.\n\nBut I do keep replaying every appointment, every little change, like I should’ve caught something sooner. I keep telling myself a better husband would’ve protected her somehow. And… I guess when I say it out loud, that does sound heavier than maybe it should.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time when you had depression like this that completely went away for at least two months?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, to be honest. I’ve had rough stretches before, but I don’t know that I’ve ever really kept track of whether it fully went away for two months or more.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. Some times I probably felt more like myself again, but “completely went away” is hard for me to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about things that are not really your fault?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I do. I don’t want to sound like I’m making everything about me, because it’s my wife who’s going through it.\n\nBut I keep replaying every appointment and every change in her health, thinking I should’ve caught it sooner or done something differently. Like if I’d been a better husband, maybe I could’ve protected her somehow.\n\nI know, rationally, that may not be fair… but it feels like my fault anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. I mean, I don’t want to make too big a deal of it, but it’s been harder than usual.\n\nI’ll be looking at blueprints I’ve read my whole life, and it’s like the lines just blur together in my head. Then I have to ask the younger guys to repeat something I should’ve understood right away. That’s… embarrassing, honestly. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
